This feature provides the single lookup\nboth options share and the rejection when the name is not a header column.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- The lookup compares the requested name with the trimmed header names by exact string equality.\n  It is case-sensitive.\n- When the name is absent, the command exits 3, writes nothing to standard output, and writes one\n  diagnostic line to standard error naming the file and the column.\n- The lookup runs after the file has been read and validated, so a file rejected for another\n  reason (empty, bad header, ragged record) reports that rejection first.\n- A name that is present proceeds to the normal profile or frequency path with exit 0.\n- Usage validation (exit 2) completes before the file is opened and therefore before this lookup.\n\n## Interfaces\n\n- Package `internal/csvdata` (or `internal/cli`) exposes a lookup returning the column index and a\n  found flag; A decimal point, a leading `+`, an exponent, or any other character\n  makes the column not integer.\n- Columns this story does not classify leave `type` empty until later type stories are built.\n  Column with no non-null value is never `integer`.\n\n## Interfaces\n\n- `internal/infer` exposes a classification function over a column's non-null trimmed values; the\n  profile writer calls it per column.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC typ001-integer-detected ===\nIntent: Columns whose non-null values all match the integer pattern, including negatives, leading zeros, padding, and nulls, are typed integer.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nimport os\nimport tempfile\n\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\ncolumns = {\n    \"plain\": [\"1\", \"-20\", \"0\", \"007\"],\n    \"padded\": [\" 5 \", \"6\", \"-7\", \"8\"],\n    \"with_nulls\": [\"1\", \"\", \"NA\", \"N/A\"],\n    \"with_null_word\": [\"10\", \"NULL\", \"null\", \"-3\"],\n}\nnames = list(columns)\ncount = len(columns[names[0]])\nbody = io.StringIO()\nwriter = csv.writer(body, lineterminator=\"\\n\")\nwriter.writerow(names)\nfor i in range(count):\n    writer.writerow([columns[k][i] for k in names])\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as d:\n    path = os.path.join(d, \"t.csv\")\n    with open(path, \"w\", newline=\"\") as f:\n        f.write(body.getvalue())\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(path)\nprint(out)\nprint(err)\nassert code == 0\nrows = {r[\"column\"]: r for r in csv.DictReader(io.StringIO(out))}\nfor name in names:\n    assert rows[name][\"type\"] == \"integer\", name\n=== END AC typ001-integer-detected ===\n\n=== AC typ001-non-integer-rejected ===\nIntent: Columns with a decimal, a plus sign, an exponent, or a FEATURE-Type-Number.md ===\n# FEATURE: Type Number\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Infer number columns for decimal values that are not all integers. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Type-Integer.md |\n| Provides    | type number |\n| Consumes    | csvdata.IsNull |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nExtend the type ladder so columns with decimal values are typed `number`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- A column that is not `integer` is `number` when every non-null trimmed value matches\n  `-?[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?`.\n- A column of only integer-shaped values stays `integer` (first match wins).\n- Forms such as `1.`, `.5`, `+1`, `1e3`, and `1,5` are not numbers.\n- Null values are ignored; a column with no non-null value is never `number`.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC typ002-number-detected ===\nIntent: Columns mixing integers and decimals, including negatives and padding and nulls, are typed number.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nimport os\nimport tempfile\n\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\ncolumns = {\n    \"mixed\": [\"1\", \"2.50\", \"-3\", \"4\"],\n    \"all_decimal\": [\"0.5\", \"-0.25\", \"3.14159\", \"10.0\"],\n    \"padded\": [\" 1.5 \", \"2\", \"3\", \"4\"],\n    \"with_nulls\": [\"1.5\", \"\", \"NA\", \"N/A\"],\n}\nnames = list(columns)\ncount = len(columns[names[0]])\nbody = io.StringIO()\nwriter = csv.writer(body, lineterminator=\"\\n\")\nwriter.writerow(names)\nfor i in range(count):\n    writer.writerow([columns[k][i] for k in names])\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as d:\n    path = os.path.join(d, \"t.csv\")\n    with open(path, \"w\", newline=\"\") as f:\n        f.write(body.getvalue())\n    code, out, err =
